#### WS-01 — Open v2.65.0; AUDIT.md re-vendor check grades by step 5, counts same-day commits and reads a consolidated span bundle

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** —

- **Change** PRE: WowAddonStandards is on master; create branch feat/2026-09-23-review-audit-remediation first (all WS items commit there). (1) standards/STANDARDS.md line 1 -> '# Ka0s WoW Addon Standard (v2.65.0, <date>)'; add a '- **v2.65.0 (<date>):**' entry at the top of '## Changelog' with numbered paragraph (1) for this item. WS-02..WS-08 append (2)..(n) to the SAME entry; nobody bumps again. (2) AUDIT.md, re-vendor bundle check (~:296-347): change `git log --since="$horizon"` to `git log --since="$horizon 00:00"`, because git fills in a bare date's time from the clock and so skips same-day commits. Replace both hard-coded grades (':335' and ':341-342' 'is a **High** finding') with: 'is a finding, graded by step 5 like every other: an unrecorded re-vendor is doc-only, so **Low**, and the entry names the `audit-review-history` MUST it fails'. Add one sentence saying step 5's table governs every grade in step 4. (3) Recorded-side loop: when a folder name carries two tags (`docs/revendor/<YYYY-MM-DD>-v<A>-v<B>/`), emit EVERY `v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+` on line 1 of its 01_DELTA.md instead of `tail -1`. Leave the single-tag and bare-dated branches unchanged. (4) standards/standards/audit-review-history.md: define the consolidated span bundle as the sanctioned record for a lapsed span. It is `docs/revendor/<date>-v<A>-v<B>/` holding 01_DELTA.md and 05_SUMMARY.md only. Line 1 of 01_DELTA.md is exactly `Delta: LibKa0s v<A> -> v<B> (span: v<A> v<...> v<B>)`, naming every tag. 05_SUMMARY.md has one line per tag, either 'carried by sweep, nothing adopted' or the adoption commit sha. Frozen bundles are never edited, and a base misstatement is corrected in the next bundle.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: the vendored-side loop walks `git log -- libs/LibKa0s tests/_kit` (both paths), so kit-only re-vendors count.

Behavioral check instead: before editing, run the CURRENT recorded-side loop over a scratch copy of AuraMaster/docs/revendor/ plus a fake span folder and confirm it records only the last tag (red). Run the amended loop and confirm it records every tag (green).

#### WS-02 — Rule the degraded composed-row conflict: hollow composers stand; host verbs write through a declared writeThrough list or refuse with one library-absent line

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** WS-01 · **Issues** PartyFrameEnhanced#14, WhatGroup#22
- **Findings** AbsorbTracker-A-23
- **Change** standards/standards/options-ui.md §1 (the hollow-composer and fall-together passage, ~:50-60): keep 'a library-absent Options stub's composers answer {}' and anti-pattern #73 (no host copies). Replace the fall-together bound with this ruling. On a library-absent load, a host verb whose write targets a composed row (enabled, locked, test mode, the combat re-lock, the launcher stub) MUST do one of two things. (a) Write through the schema seam's declared `writeThrough` path list (LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 minor 2, from LibKa0s v1.56.0 / kit revision 26). The list is data (path strings) handed to both the live instance and the runtime-completing stub, with no row, label, widget, default or validate written by hand; the value's default comes from the AceDB defaults table as before. (b) Refuse with the library-absent line. It MUST NOT raise a Lua error and MUST NOT acknowledge a write that did not land. `enable`/`disable` SHOULD take (a). Choosing (b) for them is a SHOULD deviation recorded in the host's Documented deviations. standards/standards/slash-commands.md §1: add the library-absent line, routed through the locale as one sentence with one placeholder: `%s is unavailable: the LibKa0s library did not load.` (%s = the full verb, e.g. '/wg enable'). Add: a library-absent Slash stub MAY carry exactly one library string verbatim, `LibKa0s-Slash-1.0`'s `DISABLED_LINE_FORMAT`, and MUST pin it against the live library with `Kit.assertLibraryConstant` (kit revision 26). It MUST NOT copy `FormatRow` (help rows print `cmd  desc` plainly), and minimal OnSlash dispatch is sanctioned. §7: cross-reference the sanctioned verbatim refusal line. standards/standards/library-stack.md §7 Schema row: name `writeThrough`, `normalize`, `SetMany` as minor-2 surfaces. anti-patterns.md #73: cross-reference the writeThrough route. STANDARDS.md changelog paragraph (2), citing AbsorbTracker's review item 62 / Info-6 (not plan item AT-62), PartyFrameEnhanced#14 and WhatGroup#22 (whose owner ruling takes route (b)).
- **Test first** none — docs only (standard text). The executable halves land in LK-18 (Kit.assertLibraryConstant) and LK-23 (writeThrough + reference stub).
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/WowAddonStandards; grep -n 'writeThrough' standards/standards/options-ui.md standards/standards/library-stack.md standards/standards/anti-patterns.md; grep -n 'did not load' standards/standards/slash-commands.md; grep -n 'assertLibraryConstant' standards/standards/slash-commands.md; grep -c 'v2.65.0' standards/STANDARDS.md; grep -rnE '§[0-9]+\.[0-9]+' standards/standards/*.md (expect nothing: retired notation)

#### WS-03 — Rule migration-stamp ownership: defaults declare schemaVersion 0, the runner owns the stamp, profile steps run per profile

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** WS-01
- **Findings** WHATGROUP-R-03, LootHistory-A-18, PanelMaster-A-17
- **Change** standards/standards/savedvariables.md §1: change the template (:10, :18-24) from `global = { schemaVersion = 1 }` / the current version to `global = { schemaVersion = 0 }`. Explain why in the text. AceDB's removeDefaults strips a stored value equal to its default at logout, so a default equal to the current version never persists and the first real migration is skipped (WhatGroup F-003). AceDB also backfills a declared default onto a legacy account with no stamp, which a current-version default would mask (PanelMaster febf108). 0 has neither problem. Add these rules. `NS.SCHEMA_VERSION` is the runner's target. The runner advances the stamp only past a step that returned without raising. A profile-scoped step runs for EVERY stored profile (walk the raw SV `profiles` table), or idempotently on OnProfileChanged with a per-profile stamp; it is never gated by the account-wide stamp alone (KickCD R-01, PartyFrameEnhanced R-15, PrettyChat R-03). Every step is idempotent against a fresh default profile. standards/standards/toc-file.md §2 (:55): 'MUST declare schemaVersion = 0 in defaults'. standards/standards/versioning-git.md (:8): replace 'increment schemaVersion (in defaults)' with 'add a runner step and raise NS.SCHEMA_VERSION; the defaults value stays 0'. standards/standards/open-evolutions.md (:15-24): move 'Migration-stamp ownership' to ruled at v2.65.0, naming PanelMaster, LootHistory and WhatGroup as the worked cases. STANDARDS.md changelog paragraph (3).
- **Test first** none — docs only. The rule's executable form is each addon's own red-first migration test (consumer follow-ups).
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/WowAddonStandards; grep -n 'schemaVersion = 0' standards/standards/savedvariables.md standards/standards/toc-file.md; ! grep -n 'increment schemaVersion' standards/standards/versioning-git.md; grep -n -i 'every stored profile' standards/standards/savedvariables.md; grep -n -A3 'Migration-stamp ownership' standards/standards/open-evolutions.md \| grep -i 'ruled'

#### WS-04 — Settle events-frames-taint-§1 against library-stack-§1, name LibKa0s-Core's SafeRegisterEvent as the helper, and state architecture-§4's threshold

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** WS-01
- **Findings** PRETTYCHAT-A-08, PRETTYCHAT-A-11, WHATGROUP-A-04
- **Change** standards/standards/events-frames-taint.md §1. (a) Add carve-out: an addon that embeds NO AceEvent-3.0 at all MAY carry ONE lazily created private watcher frame for non-unit boundary events (PLAYER_REGEN_DISABLED/ENABLED). The frame is fully unregistered on stand-down and listed in ## Event Subscriptions, with no register row. An addon that embeds AceEvent gets no such carve-out. (b) Name the collection helper: from LibKa0s v1.56.0, 'the single pcalled helper' is `LibKa0s-Core-1.0`'s `SafeRegisterEvent` / `SafeRegisterUnitEvent` / `SafeRegisterEvents` (LK-11), which front-gates with C_EventUtils.IsEventValid when present. The host owns the rejected list and surfaces it in the [Init] summary or a debug verb, and its Core stub carries one-rung pcall bodies. standards/standards/library-stack.md §1: re-word the PrettyChat citation (:24) to cite the new carve-out rather than contradict ef-§1. standards/standards/architecture.md §4 (:60-71): the threshold becomes 'two or more feature modules, or a feature module that registers game events and whose events a second feature module must react to'. The AceAddon object's own event handlers are not a feature module, so a shell that registers events and calls its one feature module directly is below the threshold. Keep the same-target clobber rationale. STANDARDS.md changelog paragraph (4).
- **Test first** none — docs only.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/WowAddonStandards; grep -n 'SafeRegisterEvent' standards/standards/events-frames-taint.md; grep -n -i 'embeds no AceEvent' standards/standards/events-frames-taint.md; grep -n -i 'AceAddon object' standards/standards/architecture.md; sed -n '20,30p' standards/standards/library-stack.md

#### WS-06 — Clarify reserved verbs in sub-trees, the value-hold verb, who pins a library strip, and the minimap row's CLI path polarity

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** WS-01
- **Findings** KICKCD-A-27, AbsorbTracker-A-14, PRETTYCHAT-A-26, WHATGROUP-R-15
- **Change** (1) standards/standards/slash-commands.md §2 (:31): enable/disable are reserved at the TOP level, and top-level reuse stays forbidden. Under a feature noun's sub-tree (`/<slash> <noun> enable <id>`) they MAY toggle that noun's items, because the first token already disambiguates (KickCD /kcd spells). (2) standards/standards/options-ui.md §15 and preview-mode.md: an addon whose unlocked view is its preview ships no `test` verb. A one-shot value hold, if kept, lives at `/<slash> debug hold <value> [secs]`, which is not a test mode. (3) options-ui.md §13 Testing: for a strip the library draws (O.TabStrip, O.RenderTabbedSchema), the library suite pins the wrap invariance and a consumer MUST NOT duplicate it (testing-§8). A host-drawn strip is pinned by the host. (4) standards/standards/launcher.md §3, cross-referenced from options-ui.md §15: storage stays LibDBIcon's `minimap.hide`, and a stored `shown` key is still forbidden (anti-pattern #81). The minimap row's schema path, which is the CLI name, MUST read in the row's own shown sense: `<root>.minimap.shown`. It is a row whose get/set closures invert onto `minimap.hide`, so `/<slash> get <root>.minimap.shown` answers true while the button is visible. Commencement: each addon's next release. No SavedVariables migration, because the stored key does not move. STANDARDS.md changelog paragraph (6).
- **Test first** none — docs only.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/WowAddonStandards; grep -n -i 'sub-tree' standards/standards/slash-commands.md; grep -n 'debug hold' standards/standards/options-ui.md standards/standards/preview-mode.md; grep -n 'minimap.shown' standards/standards/launcher.md standards/standards/options-ui.md; grep -n -i 'library suite pins' standards/standards/options-ui.md

#### LK-11 — Core: SafeRegisterEvent / SafeRegisterUnitEvent / SafeRegisterEvents, the collection's pcalled registration helper ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** LK-10, LK-04, WS-04
- **Findings** AuraMaster-R-05
- **Change** LibKa0s/Core.lua, still minor 8 (LK-10 bumped it; no second bump). Add three lib-level members. `lib.SafeRegisterEvent(target, event, handler, rejected) -> boolean`: if `C_EventUtils and C_EventUtils.IsEventValid` exists and answers false, the name is rejected without a call; otherwise it runs `pcall(target.RegisterEvent, target, event, handler)`, which works for an AceEvent-embedded object and for a Frame. `lib.SafeRegisterUnitEvent(frame, event, rejected, unit1, unit2) -> boolean` does the same through RegisterUnitEvent. `lib.SafeRegisterEvents(target, events, handler, rejected) -> number registered` walks an array. `rejected` is an optional caller-owned array, appended once per name with no duplicates. The library keeps no state and prints nothing; the host surfaces the list (events-frames-taint-§1, WS-04). A Bus-stamped target still records the registration, because the call goes through `target:RegisterEvent`. Keep each function at CCN 10 or below. Extend docs/api/Core/version-8-docs.md: the three members, Since 8, and a 'Degradation' note that a host's Core stub carries one-rung bodies (pcall, no front gate) so Kit.assertSurfaceParity stays green. Regenerate members-8.json. CHANGELOG entry.
- **Test first** Red first in tests/test_core.lua (586 lines), using the kit's M.__badEvents plus the C_EventUtils mock from LK-04: a bad name among three leaves the other two registered and the bad one in `rejected` once; with IsEventValid present the bad name is rejected with no RegisterEvent call; with C_EventUtils = nil the pcall path records it; SafeRegisterUnitEvent on a frame likewise; SafeRegisterEvents returns the count. Each carries '-- red under: a bare RegisterEvent'.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/LibKa0s; B=~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded; $B luacheck .; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua; grep -n 'SafeRegisterEvent' docs/api/Core/members-8.json; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list \| diff --strip-trailing-cr - docs/test-cases.md; $B lizard -l lua -x './libs/*' -x './tests/_kit/*' -C 15 -w . #### LK-19 — DebugLog minor 13: batched buffer trim instead of table.remove(1) per line at the cap ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** LK-02, WS-07
- **Findings** LibKa0s-R-10
- **Change** LibKa0s/DebugLog.lua:623: D.buffer stays a public ordered array, because tests/test_debuglog.lua:87-142 and AbsorbTracker/BankLedger tests index it. So no ring buffer. Once #D.buffer exceeds MAX_BUFFER + 64, compact by moving the newest MAX_BUFFER lines down in one pass. BufferSize/LastLine/FindLine/CopyText read only the newest MAX_BUFFER lines, so a reader never sees more than the cap. MAX_BUFFER stays 1500, which WS-07 makes the standard's number. Bump `MINOR` 12 -> 13. docs/api/DebugLog/version-13-docs.md, which states the 64-line slack and that D.buffer may briefly hold up to 1564 raw entries while every public reader answers 1500; version-12 Superseded. README row. members-13.json. CHANGELOG entry.
- **Test first** Characterization first in tests/test_debuglog.lua (892 lines), before editing: record BufferSize, LastLine, FindLine and CopyText at 1499/1500/1501/1600 lines, and '1501st line drops the first'. All must stay green after the change. Then add a red case: 1564 adds cost at most one compaction (count table moves via a wrapped table.remove spy), which is red under per-line remove.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/LibKa0s; B=~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded; $B luacheck .; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list \| diff --strip-trailing-cr - docs/test-cases.md; $B lizard -l lua -x './libs/*' -x './tests/_kit/*' -C 15 -w . #### LK-22 — Schema minor 2: instanceId forwarding, row.normalize, and SetMany (all-or-nothing batch, one bracket line, one announce)

- **Effort** L · **Depends on** LK-02 · **Issues** ConsumableMaster#39, MultiMeters#52, KickCD#22, AuraMaster#21
- **Findings** LibKa0s-R-14
- **Change** LibKa0s/Schema.lua. (1) R-14: S.Get calls `row.get(instanceId)` (:358); S.ApplyDefault(row, instanceId) forwards the id to S.Set (:497-502). (2) `row.normalize(value, rid)`, after validate passes and before store: it answers the value to store, or `nil, why`, which refuses with INVALID. AuraMaster and ConsumableMaster are the two consumers, so library-stack-§7 bar 2 is cleared. (3) `S.SetMany(entries, opts)`, where entries = { {path=, value=}, ... } and opts = { instanceId=, act=, scope= }. Phase 1 resolves, validates and normalizes every entry and returns `false, err, why, index` before any store. Phase 2 stores each entry in order; when opts.act is given it does so inside one bracket, so one `[Set] <act> <scope>: N rows` line; otherwise each write logs its own line. Each row's onChange runs. Then the descriptor's optional `announceBatch(writes, rid)` is called once with an array of {row, path, value}, or, without it, `announce` runs per write. Returns true. Extract a shared `prepareWrite(row, path, value, instanceId)` used by S.Set and SetMany so each stays at CCN 10 or below. Update the header's 'deliberately does not do' paragraph, since normalize and the all-or-nothing batch are no longer excluded. Bump `MINOR` 1 -> 2. docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md (Current, Since 2; the stub table adds SetMany with the same all-or-nothing semantics and log-silence). Its 'Adoption notes' gains per-host mappings: KickCD (row.section read by announce; SESSION/GLOBAL paths as rows with get/set; the master switch as the `enabled` row's onChange, which runs before announce), ConsumableMaster (validate-coerce into normalize; SetManyAndRefresh -> SetMany + announceBatch), MultiMeters (SetByPaths -> SetMany), AuraMaster (partial adoption now possible with normalize). version-1 Superseded. README row. members-2.json (lib-level unchanged; the doc says instance stubs gain SetMany). CHANGELOG entry.
- **Test first** Red first in a new tests/test_schema_batch.lua (test_schema.lua is 1233 lines), declared in tests/run.lua: SetMany with one invalid entry stores nothing and returns index; an unknown path is refused whole; a valid batch stores all, runs every onChange, calls announceBatch once, and with act logs exactly one bracket line with N = rows changed; normalize's value is stored, and normalize answering nil,why refuses; Get passes instanceId to row.get; ApplyDefault(row, id) reaches Set with id. Extend tests/test_schema.lua's referenceStub with SetMany and pin it against a live instance with the two-table T.assertSurfaceParity.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/LibKa0s; B=~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded; $B luacheck .; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list \| diff --strip-trailing-cr - docs/test-cases.md; $B lizard -l lua -x './libs/*' -x './tests/_kit/*' -C 15 -w . #### LK-23 — Schema writeThrough: a declared path list the live seam and the degraded stub both store without a row

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** LK-22, WS-02 · **Issues** PartyFrameEnhanced#14, WhatGroup#22
- **Findings** AbsorbTracker-A-02, AbsorbTracker-A-03
- **Change** LibKa0s/Schema.lua, still minor 2. The descriptor gains `writeThrough` (an array of path strings; the instance builds a set once). In S.Set, when no row is indexed for a path in that set, the value is stored raw via writeTarget/lib.Write (a copy), with no validate, normalize or onChange. `announce` is then called with a synthetic row `{ path = path, writeThrough = true }`, built once per path so no allocation per write. It logs like any write and returns true. A path with a row always takes the row. This covers a partial load (Schema present, Options absent). docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md: 'The degradation stub' prescribes that the runtime-completing stub takes the same `writeThrough` list and stores it the same way while refusing every other row-less path. This is the WS-02 route (a) for host verbs writing composed Master-controls rows (enabled, locked, test mode) on a library-absent load; composers stay hollow and host copies stay forbidden (#73). Adoption notes: AbsorbTracker (writeThrough {enabled, locked}; delete composeBlock and the host copies; pin full count, degraded count and the named composer gap), PartyFrameEnhanced (#14: writeThrough for the Slash.lua:117/:152 and Preview.lua:134 paths), WhatGroup (#22: owner ruling takes route (b), the library-absent line, no writeThrough). tests/test_schema.lua referenceStub gains writeThrough. CHANGELOG entry.
- **Test first** Red first in tests/test_schema_batch.lua: with writeThrough {'enabled'} and no 'enabled' row, Set('enabled', false) stores false and announces with row.writeThrough == true (red under: NOT_FOUND refusal); an unlisted row-less path is still refused; a listed path WITH a row goes through its validate. Then the referenceStub: the same three cases against the stub, plus parity with a live instance on store order.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/LibKa0s; B=~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded; $B luacheck .; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua; grep -n 'writeThrough' docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md; $B lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list \| diff --strip-trailing-cr - docs/test-cases.md; $B lizard -l lua -x './libs/*' -x './tests/_kit/*' -C 15 -w . - **Change** LibKa0s/Options.lua registerMain/CreateOptionsPanel (:1322-1387). Under InCombatLockdown(), park the registration: store the request, and register PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED on a library-private frame only while something is parked. #### LK-28 — RenderTabbedSchema moves to OptionsTabs.lua and gains host tabs, a disabled notice and a chrome hook; PageBanner gains an action button ⚠

- **Effort** L · **Depends on** LK-27, LK-25
- **Findings** AuraMaster-R-04
- **Change** Move O.RenderTabbedSchema (LibKa0s/OptionsWidgets.lua:3820-3921 with its doc block) into OptionsTabs.lua's __AttachTabs. Change Options.lua:1471 to `lib.__AttachTabs(O, d)` so it reaches d.rowsForPage. OptionsWidgets keeps an untabbed fallback `O.RenderTabbedSchema` (RenderRows over the page, at most 10 lines) that the Tabs attach overrides, so a partial copy without OptionsTabs still renders. This is a first peel toward LibKa0s #16 (OptionsWidgets over cap). New optional fields on a 5th argument `opts` (no existing caller changes): `tabs` (an array of {key, label, render = function(ctx)} for bespoke non-row tabs, each placed before a named group via `before`); `disabledFor(cfg)` plus `disabledNotice` (a string or function), which renders the notice in place of rows when disabledFor answers true; `chrome(ctx)`, called after the strip and before rows. O.PageBanner gains an optional right-half `action = { text, tooltip, onClick }`, for the options-ui-§14 picker+create band. Check against anti-pattern #55: each field serves AuraMaster's seven pages, and AbsorbTracker (settings/UnitPanel.lua:327), KickCD (settings/Panel_Render.lua:208), ConsumableMaster (settings/General.lua:402) and MultiMeters (settings/Columns.lua:321) hand-build the same strip shape, so no field is per-consumer. Minors were already bumped by LK-26/LK-27 in this release. Extend the Options version doc (fields Since 31/4). CHANGELOG entry.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: disabledFor answering true draws the disabledNotice ABOVE the rows and renders the rows disabled (RenderRows opts.disabled plus ctx.__renderDisabled around bespoke render); it does not replace them. A `tabs` entry whose key equals a schema group takes that group's place in the strip and is handed the group's rows (render(ctx, rows)). Library tests pin both, so AM-17 adopts without changing its disabled-state characterization.

Then red-first cases in tests/test_options_tabs.lua: a host tab renders through its callback and sits before the named group; disabledFor true shows the notice and no rows; chrome is called once per render after the strip; PageBanner action draws a button whose click calls onClick; with OptionsTabs absent (loader without the file) RenderTabbedSchema renders untabbed without raising.

#### AT-01 — Schema degradation stub catches up to LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 minor 2: SetMany, normalize, instanceId forwarding and the writeThrough store

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** LK-22, LK-23, LK-33
- **Findings** —
- **Change** settings/Schema.lua, HostSchemaStub() (:82-219) only; the descriptor is not touched here (AT-08 adopts writeThrough). (1) Extract a stub-local `prepareWrite(row, path, value, id)` from S.Set (:159-183): resolve root/first/rid for a stored row, run row.validate (refuse `false, 'AbsorbTracker: invalid value for '..path, why`), then row.normalize(value, rid) when present (a nil answer refuses the same way with its why), then the `nowhere to store` refusal. It returns `true, prep` where prep = {row, parts, root, first, rid, value}. Add `commit(prep)`, which stores (row.set or stubLib.Write of a copy) and runs row.onChange. S.Set becomes FindRow -> prepareWrite -> commit -> d.announce, and S.Set, prepareWrite and commit each stay at CCN 10 or below. Today's S.Set is the addon's max at CCN 14. (2) writeThrough: in stubLib.New build once `local through = {}` from `d.writeThrough` (an array of path strings), mapping each path to a cached synthetic row `{ path = p, writeThrough = true }`. In S.Set, when FindRow finds no row and `through[path]` exists, resolve the root, stubLib.Write a copy with no validate, normalize or onChange, call d.announce(synthetic, path, value, rid) and return true. Every other row-less path is still refused with the existing line. (3) SetMany(entries, opts): phase 1 runs FindRow/writeThrough lookup plus prepareWrite for every entry and returns `false, err, why, index` before any store; phase 2 commits in order. Then call `d.announceBatch(writes, rid)` once if the descriptor has it, else d.announce per write. It is log-silent (no bracket line) and returns true. (4) S.Get(path, id) calls `row.get(id)`; S.ApplyDefault(row, id) forwards id to S.Set. (5) Update the stub's doc comment (:66-81) to cite LibKa0s docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md 'The degradation stub' and name SetMany/writeThrough. This is the parity churn the v1.56.0 payload brings. The item can land BEFORE RV-AT: Kit.assertSurfaceParity is one-directional (live must be a subset of stub), so an extra stub member is green on v1.55.0.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: this item lands BEFORE RV-AT (it is in M2, ahead of the re-vendor commit) so the re-vendor lands green; it must pass on both the v1.55.0 payload and the v1.56.0 dry-run.
- **Test first** Red-first in tests/test_schema.lua, new section '-- the degradation stub, Schema minor 2'. Build a stub instance from `loadDegraded().__schemaLib:New{ rows = {...}, resolveRoot = function() return store, 1 end, announce = spy, writeThrough = { 'enabled' } }` over a local store with two rows (one carrying validate). Cases: (a) SetMany with entry 2 invalid returns false, 'invalid', why, 2, and entry 1 is NOT stored (-- red under: committing inside phase 1); (b) a valid SetMany stores both, fires each onChange once, and announces twice, or once via announceBatch when given; (c) S.Set('enabled', false) with no row stores raw, fires no onChange, announces with row.writeThrough == true, and returns true (-- red under: dropping the through lookup); (d) S.Set('nope', 1) is still refused; (e) row.get receives the id passed to S.Get. The existing 'parity: the Schema stub's instance carries every member of a live instance' case in tests/test_surface_parity.lua must be green after RV-AT (red on a v1.56.0 payload without this item). Regenerate docs/test-cases.md with `lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list > docs/test-cases.md` and the README Tests badge in the same commit.

#### AM-01 — Pre-re-vendor: remove the lone CR in tests/page_helpers.lua and clear every red the v1.56.0 kit dry-run raises

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** LK-06, LK-33
- **Findings** AuraMaster-A-11
- **Change** RUNS BEFORE RV-AM, which has to be green when it lands (LK-06 and LK-33 consumer follow-ups). (1) tests/page_helpers.lua:80 ends \r\r\n. Delete the first CR only: `perl -pi -e 's/\r\r\n/\r\n/' tests/page_helpers.lua`. Then run `git add --renormalize tests/page_helpers.lua`. Before this, git classified the file -text because of the lone CR, so the index holds a CRLF blob with 272 CRs. After it, `git ls-files --eol tests/page_helpers.lua` must print `i/lf w/crlf attr/text=auto eol=crlf`. Renormalize is correct here even though line-endings-§7 says 'never renormalize', because that rule assumes a correct index and this index is wrong. (2) Dry-run the v1.56.0 payload without touching the repo: `T=$(mktemp -d); git archive HEAD \| tar -x -C $T; mkdir $T/_lk; git -C ../LibKa0s archive v1.56.0 LibKa0s testkit \| tar -x -C $T/_lk; rm -rf $T/libs/LibKa0s $T/tests/_kit; cp -r $T/_lk/LibKa0s $T/libs/LibKa0s; cp -r $T/_lk/testkit $T/tests/_kit; (cd $T && git init -q && git add -A && ka0s-bounded lua5.1 tests/run.lua)`. The git init is needed because test_eol, test_prose and test_layout_cap read the tracked set. test_vendor_sync is expected red there (the provenance line still says v1.55.0) and belongs to RV-AM; every other red is fixed in THIS commit, in addon code or addon tests only, never in libs/ or tests/_kit/. Known kit-26 flips to check: CreateFrame starts shown, so a case that relied on hidden-by-default gets the Hide() its production path performs and no assertion is weakened. AceDB fake CopyProfile/DeleteProfile raise on the active or a missing profile, and SetProfile strips defaults (tests/test_bus.lua:126-128, tests/test_bulklog.lua:129-146, tests/test_blizzardframes.lua:128, tests/test_containermanager.lua:480-520); a fixture that copies onto the active profile gets a real source. EventRegistry is recorded. Lone CRs. Store-root prose: an emulation of the 92-word list over docs/automated-tests/README.md, RESULTS.md and docs/perf-analysis/README.md found no hit on 2026-09-23. Case names now carry §. (3) tests/test_container.lua:299: reword the comment 'Kit frames start hidden: show it first, or the IsShown assertion below could never fail' so it does not depend on the kit's default, e.g. 'Shown first, whatever the kit's CreateFrame default, so the IsShown assertion below can fail.' List every dry-run red and its fix in the commit body.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: this item lands BEFORE RV-AM (it is in M2, ahead of the re-vendor commit) so the re-vendor lands green; it must pass on both the v1.55.0 payload and the v1.56.0 dry-run.

For each other dry-run red, the red case itself is the failing test, fixed without weakening an assertion.

#### BL-01 — Retention prune rides AceTimer and latches only when it runs; wow_mock stops no-opping C_Timer.After (PRE-RV: lands before RV-BL) ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** LK-33
- **Findings** BankLedger-A-03, BankLedger-R-05
- **Change** LANDS BEFORE RV-BL (the LK-33 dry-run pre-fix named for BankLedger; green under both kit 25 and kit 26). (1) tests/wow_mock.lua:608-617: delete `M.C_Timer.After = function() end` and rewrite the comment block. The kit's recording After stands (testing-§1, record rather than no-op). The prune stays out of suites because nothing fires it: every mocks.__fireTimers caller (tests/test_browser.lua:447-484, tests/test_ledger_settling.lua:164-181) resets mocks.__timers first. Also check core/ItemSetup.lua:67 (C_Timer.After(0.4, cb) in LoadItem), which now records too. If a suite asserts an empty queue after a LoadItem, reset mocks.__timers in that suite's setup. Never re-add the override. (2) core/BankLedger.lua:216-229 addon:OnEnterWorld becomes: `local st = NS.State; if st.cleanupDone or st.cleanupPending then return end; if not self.ScheduleTimer then return end; st.cleanupPending = self:ScheduleTimer(function() st.cleanupPending = nil; if NS.IsStoodDown and NS.IsStoodDown() then return end; st.cleanupDone = true; if NS.Database and NS.Database.PruneOld then NS.Database:PruneOld() end end, 5)`. The IsStoodDown check stays as a belt, and the latch is set only when the prune actually runs. Rewrite the :221-224 comment: the timer is an AceTimer handle that NS.StandDown's CancelAllTimers cancels, and a stand-down postpones the prune to the next PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD instead of skipping it for the session. (3) core/State.lua: declare `State.cleanupPending = nil` beside cleanupDone, with a comment (session-only AceTimer handle). (4) NS.StandDown step 1 (core/BankLedger.lua:151-155): after ad:CancelAllTimers(), set `NS.State.cleanupPending = nil` so the next PEW after a stand-up re-arms. (5) docs/performance.md:47 row: the login deferral is an AceTimer that the stand-down cancels, and the once-per-session latch is set when the prune runs.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: this item lands BEFORE RV-BL (it is in M2, ahead of the re-vendor commit) so the re-vendor lands green; it must pass on both the v1.55.0 payload and the v1.56.0 dry-run.

In tests/test_disabled.lua, extend 'disabled: no timer, ticker or OnUpdate is left armed' (:184-203): reset NS.State.cleanupDone/cleanupPending, then call NS.addon:OnEnterWorld() before disable(). With step (1) alone the case goes red, because the C_Timer.After entry survives CancelAllTimers. Add a red-under comment naming C_Timer.After. Add a case 'disabled: a stand-down inside the prune window postpones the prune rather than canceling it': cleanupDone=false, OnEnterWorld, disable, assert #mocks.__timers()==0 and cleanupDone==false, enable, OnEnterWorld, mocks.__fireTimers(), assert a PruneOld spy ran exactly once and cleanupDone==true. It is red under the latch-before-timer shape. Add 'OnEnterWorld arms the retention prune once per session' (two PEWs, one queued timer). That is +2 cases. Regenerate docs/test-cases.md (lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list > docs/test-cases.md) and move the README test count if it shows one.

#### BL-02 — Schema degradation stub gains SetMany (all-or-nothing batch), ahead of the LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 minor 2 parity check (PRE-RV: lands before RV-BL)

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** LK-22, LK-33
- **Findings** —
- **Change** LANDS BEFORE RV-BL. Kit.assertSurfaceParity is one-directional (the stub must carry every live member), so adding the member early is harmless under v1.55.0. After RV-BL, the existing parity case at tests/test_surface_parity.lua:315-320 reddens without it. settings/Schema.lua stub:New (:502-561): extract a local `store(row, path, value)` (resolve root, row.set or stub.Write(copy), onChange) shared by R.Set and the new R.SetMany, so each stays at CCN 10 or below. Add `function R.SetMany(entries, opts)`, mirroring docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md at the v1.56.0 tag, log-silent like the rest of the stub. Phase 1 walks every entry and stores nothing. It answers `false, words('NOT_FOUND', path), nil, i` for an unknown path, `false, words('INVALID', path), why, i` when validate refuses, and `false, words('NO_ROOT', path), nil, i` with no store. Phase 2: when opts and opts.act, depth = depth + 1 around the writes so the sweep veto reads the same bracket. It stores each entry in order through store(), which runs each row's onChange. Then it calls `d.announceBatch(writes)` when the descriptor carries one, and otherwise d.announce per write. It returns true. Update the stub header's 'Trimmed' paragraph (:450-457): SetMany is carried, with no caller in this addon, for parity. Do not adopt SetMany in any host path.  Upstream: this is the BankLedger consumer follow-up of LK-22 (LibKa0s Schema minor 2 adds SetMany to the instance surface and the docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md stub table); the stub SetMany mirrors LK-22's all-or-nothing semantics and log-silence exactly, and the post-RV-BL parity check is against the LK-22 surface.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: this item lands BEFORE RV-BL (it is in M2, ahead of the re-vendor commit) so the re-vendor lands green; it must pass on both the v1.55.0 payload and the v1.56.0 dry-run.
- **Test first** Test first, in tests/test_surface_parity.lua, Schema arm beside :315: 'LibKa0s-Schema degraded: the stub SetMany is all-or-nothing'. Load the degraded arm. Call SetMany({ {path='settings.visibility', value='never'}, {path='settings.visibility', value='bogus'} }) (or any row whose validate refuses; pick one by reading S.Schema's validate fields). It answers false with index 2, and the stored visibility is unchanged. A valid two-entry batch stores both and runs each onChange once (spy NS.Util.ApplyVisibility). It is red before the change because SetMany is nil. Add one case, regenerate docs/test-cases.md.

#### WG-01 — Unregister the EventRegistry 'SetItemRef' callback at stand-down, re-register at stand-up, and make the disabled survey see it ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** LK-33
- **Findings** WHATGROUP-A-01, WHATGROUP-A-02, WHATGROUP-A-03
- **Change** core/WhatGroup.lua:108-114: pull the registration into `local function registerLinkCallback()` (body: `if ADDON_LINK_TYPE then EventRegistry:RegisterCallback('SetItemRef', function(_, linkArg) onDetailsLinkClick(linkArg) end, WhatGroup) end`). Keep the file-load call where it is (the taint reason in the :45-53 comment still holds), and keep the `else hooksecurefunc('SetItemRef', onDetailsLinkClick)` fallback at file load. NS.StandDown (:329-349): add `if ADDON_LINK_TYPE and EventRegistry then EventRegistry:UnregisterCallback('SetItemRef', WhatGroup) end` next to the four UnregisterEvent calls. NS.StandUp (:365-372): call registerLinkCallback() after registerFeatureEvents(self). The registry keeps one callback per (event, owner), so a repeat call replaces the old one and cannot stack. Keep the `NS.IsStoodDown()` gate in onDetailsLinkClick because the hooksecurefunc fallback still needs it. Rewrite the :96-99 comment: the gate now serves only the fallback that cannot be unhooked; the EventRegistry route is really unregistered (slash-commands-§7, anti-pattern #85). Also rewrite the :290-292 comment in registerFeatureEvents, which says the chat-link callback cannot be unhooked. tests/test_disabled.lua:76-82 regNames: after the kit survey and rawRegs, append `callback:<event>` for every owner in `mock.EventRegistry.__callbacks('SetItemRef')` (the local wow_mock fake at tests/wow_mock.lua:668-694). Update the :56-63 header comment. The expected sets in 'disabled 1' (:120) and 'disabled 9' (:409) gain `callback:SetItemRef`. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:158-161 and :238-241: only the two hooksecurefunc rows survive the stand-down; move the :170 EventRegistry row into the set that is gone while disabled (WHATGROUP-A-02). docs/smoke-tests.md: add the in-client check listed under smoke. This item works with either kit revision, so it can land before RV-WG. The LK-33 dry-run names it the WhatGroup fix that must land before the re-vendor.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: this item lands BEFORE RV-WG (it is in M2, ahead of the re-vendor commit) so the re-vendor lands green; it must pass on both the v1.55.0 payload and the v1.56.0 dry-run.
- **Test first** Test first: extend regNames as described and run the suite. 'disabled 3: the registration set is EMPTY' (tests/test_disabled.lua:136) must go red, naming callback:SetItemRef. That is the survivor made visible (WHATGROUP-A-03). Add '-- red under: dropping the EventRegistry:UnregisterCallback in NS.StandDown' above it. Add a case in tests/test_lifecycle.lua near :227: 'lifecycle: disable drops the SetItemRef callback and enable restores exactly one' asserts `mock.EventRegistry.__callbacks('SetItemRef')[NS.addon]` is nil after /wg disable and non-nil after /wg enable, and that one owner key exists. Then make the core change so both go green. Regenerate docs/test-cases.md with `lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list > docs/test-cases.md` and the README Tests badge in the same commit.

#### BL-03 — Stand-down drops the capture context (L:DropContext): openContext, lastSnapshot, _settleSince and the session end with the latch ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** RV-BL, BL-01, BL-00
- **Findings** BankLedger-R-01
- **Change** modules/Ledger.lua: after L:CloseContext (:803-815), add `function L:DropContext()`. It reads `local context = NS.State.openContext`, calls self:CancelPendingReconcile(), sets `NS.State.openContext, NS.State.lastSnapshot, L._settleSince = nil, nil, nil`, and when context was set calls fireSessionChanged(false, context). There is NO Reconcile/flush, because movements made at the moment of disabling must not be captured. Docblock: this is the stand-down's context teardown, the only path besides CloseContext and disarmGuildBankIfGone that clears these fields, and it needs no event. core/BankLedger.lua NS.StandDown: a new step '3b. THE CAPTURE CONTEXT', after the RefreshUpvalues step (:164) and BEFORE the bus-target loop, because SessionWindow's __ev must still be subscribed to receive SessionChanged(false) and run SW:EndSession, which clears State.sessionActive (modules/SessionWindow.lua:137): `if NS.Ledger and NS.Ledger.DropContext then NS.Ledger:DropContext() end`. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md '### What stands down, and what survives' (~:336-370): add the capture context (open context, baseline, settle window, banking session) to what stands down.
- **Test first** Test first, tests/test_disabled.lua, +2 cases with red-under comments ('red under: removing the DropContext call from NS.StandDown'). (A) 'disabled at the bank: a movement made while disabled is not recorded after re-enable': mocks.__fire('BANKFRAME_OPENED'), disable(), mutate the bag and bank contents with tests/ledger_support.lua's helpers, enable(), fire BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED and mocks.__fireTimers(), assert #NS.db.global.ledger is unchanged. (B) 'disabled at the bank: the stand-down disarms the context and ends the session': after disable() assert NS.State.openContext == nil, NS.State.lastSnapshot == nil, NS.Ledger._settleSince == nil, NS.State.sessionActive == false. After enable() away from a bank, firing PLAYER_MONEY queues no timer (#mocks.__timers() == 0). Both are red at HEAD (the verify_reason repro). Regenerate docs/test-cases.md.

#### BL-10 — Library-absent /bl enable\|disable write settings.enabled through the Schema seam's writeThrough (WS-02 route a); the stub DisabledLine format is pinned to the library's bytes ⚠

It is WS-02 route (a) for the one composed row a host verb writes. settings.locked has no verb. Test mode and the debug console are session state switched through LT:SetTestMode and NS.DebugLog, never the seam. Pass `writeThrough = S.WRITE_THROUGH` in the SchemaLib:New descriptor (:566-579). The live Schema minor 2 honors it on a partial load (Schema present, Options absent). (2) The stub:New (:502-561) builds a set from d.writeThrough once, plus one synthetic row per path `{ path = p, writeThrough = true }` built once. In R.Set (and SetMany's phase 1), when FindRow answers nil and the path is in the set: resolve the root (NO_ROOT if missing), stub.Write(root, path, copy(value)), d.announce(synthetic, path, value), return true. Every other row-less path still answers NOT_FOUND. (3) settings/Slash.lua Sl:CliEnabled (:213-215), shared by both arms: after Sl:CliSet(...), call `if NS.ReevaluateEnabled then NS.ReevaluateEnabled() end`. A write-through store runs no onChange, and ReevaluateEnabled fires only on a real edge, so the full load is unaffected. The degraded branch (:291-) overrides Sl:CliEnabled, because its CliSet prints the CLI-unavailable line: `local ok, err = NS.Schema:Set(ENABLED_PATH, on)`. On a refusal it prints err, never raises and never acknowledges. Otherwise it calls NS.ReevaluateEnabled() and prints `('%s = %s'):format(ENABLED_PATH, tostring(on))`. Comment why the degraded arm carries its own body. (4) Degraded Sl:DisabledLine (:357-360): hoist the literal to `local DISABLED_LINE_FORMAT = '%s is disabled \226\128\148 enable it with \|cFFFFFF00%s\|r'`, published as `Sl.__DISABLED_LINE_FORMAT` (the __ prefix keeps it out of public parity), and build the line from it. (5) Docs: docs/schema.md 'degradation stub' and docs/slash-dispatch.md degraded section say that enable/disable keep working without the library by write-through. No Documented deviations row is needed, because route (a) is taken.

#### BL-11 — Migration stamp per standard v2.65.0: defaults declare schemaVersion = 0; the runner walks steps and stamps after each success ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** RV-BL, BL-05, WS-03, BL-00
- **Findings** —
- **Change** WS-03 consumer follow-up. (1) defaults/Global.lua:8-22: replace the 'DELIBERATELY NOT DECLARED' comment with `schemaVersion = 0,` and a short comment. 0 is never a real version, so AceDB's logout strip never removes a real stamp, and AceDB's backfill of 0 onto a legacy unstamped store reads as 'unstamped' rather than masking it (savedvariables-§1, v2.65.0). (2) core/Database.lua:30-61 NS:RunMigrations: add a module-level `NS.MIGRATIONS = { [2] = function(g) <strip vendorPrice, return rows touched> end }`. The runner reads `local from = tonumber(g.schemaVersion) or 0`, returns when from >= NS.SCHEMA_VERSION, and treats 0 (and nil) as unstamped: `local v = math.max(from, 1)`. That covers both a legacy v1 store and a fresh install, because a step over an empty ledger is a no-op. For each target = v+1..NS.SCHEMA_VERSION it runs `rows = rows + NS.MIGRATIONS[target](g)` and then sets `g.schemaVersion = target`. A raising step propagates and leaves the stamp at the last completed version. Keep the [Migrate] debug line. There is no profile scope: this addon stores only db.global (the savedvariables-§2 register row), so the per-profile rule has nothing to walk. Say so in the docblock. (3) settings/Slash.lua Sl:ResetEverything: after the defaults merge, call `NS:RunMigrations()` so a wiped store is re-stamped rather than reading v0 until the next login. (4) docs/ARCHITECTURE.md / docs/schema.md: the SavedVariables stamp paragraph.
- **Test first** Test first, tests/test_database.lua. Rewrite 'Database: schemaVersion is NOT a shipped AceDB default' (:505-513) to 'Database: defaults declare schemaVersion = 0 (savedvariables-§1)', asserting NS.defaults.global.schemaVersion == 0. It is red today. Add 'RunMigrations walks an AceDB-backfilled 0 with vendorPrice rows to v2 and strips them'. Add 'RunMigrations leaves the stamp at the last completed step when a step raises' (swap NS.MIGRATIONS[2] for a raiser, pcall, assert the stamp is unchanged, restore). Add 'ResetEverything leaves the store stamped at NS.SCHEMA_VERSION'. Keep :375 (nil treated as v1) and :479 (a future version is left alone). Regenerate docs/test-cases.md.
- **Verify** cd /mnt/d/Profile/Users/Tushar/Documents/GIT/BankLedger && ~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded luacheck . && ~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded lua5.1 tests/run.lua && lua5.1 tests/run.lua --list \| diff - docs/test-cases.md && lizard -l lua -x './libs/*' -x './tests/_kit/*' -C 15 -w . #### PF-08 — Migration runner per WS-03: schemaVersion 0 in defaults, NS.SCHEMA_VERSION is the target, profile steps run over every stored profile, stamp only after success ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-PF, PF-00
- **Findings** PartyFrameEnhanced-R-15
- **Change** defaults/Profile.lua:134 `schemaVersion = 1` -> `schemaVersion = 0`, with a comment: AceDB strips a value equal to its default at logout, so a current-version default never persists (WS-03). core/Database.lua: add `NS.SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` (the shape v0.1.0 ships); SCHEMA_STEPS rows become `{ to = N, scope = 'profile'\|'global', apply = function(tbl) ... end }`; publish `NS.__schemaSteps = SCHEMA_STEPS` for tests only. RunMigrations: `local stamp = g.schemaVersion or 0`; for each step with `stamp < step.to`: a 'profile' step runs `step.apply(p)` for every table in `NS.db.profiles` (AceDB's raw stored profiles, which include the active one) or `{ NS.db.profile }` on the no-AceDB path (:21-27), a 'global' step runs `step.apply(g)`; the whole step runs under pcall; on failure log it ungated through NS.DebugLog:Add('Migrate', ...), print one line `L["Settings migration to v%d failed; your settings were left as they were"]` and stop without stamping; on success `stamp = step.to; g.schemaVersion = stamp` and NS.Debug('Migrate', 'v%d -> v%d (%d profiles)'). After the walk, `if stamp < NS.SCHEMA_VERSION then g.schemaVersion = NS.SCHEMA_VERSION end`. Comment: a raw stored profile has defaults stripped, so every step reads with a fallback and is idempotent against a fresh default profile. Keep RunMigrations under CCN 10 by lifting `targetsFor(step)`. docs/schema.md :85, :112, :117: default 0, stamp 1 after the first run, the per-profile rule.
- **Test first** Red first in tests/test_database.lua: re-pin :17-27 to 'InitDB stamps NS.SCHEMA_VERSION over a default of 0' (NS.defaults.global.schemaVersion == 0 and NS.db.global.schemaVersion == NS.SCHEMA_VERSION after InitDB). New case with a test-local injected step: create profile 'Second', set g.schemaVersion = 1, push `{ to = 2, scope = 'profile', apply = function(p) p.__migrated = true end }` into NS.__schemaSteps with NS.SCHEMA_VERSION = 2, run NS:RunMigrations(), assert both Default and Second carry __migrated and the stamp is 2 (`-- red under: step.apply(NS.db.profile) only`); a raising step leaves the stamp at 1 and prints one line; a second run is a no-op. Restore SCHEMA_STEPS, SCHEMA_VERSION, the stamp and delete 'Second' afterwards. Regenerate docs/test-cases.md and the badge.

#### PF-11 — Adopt LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 (minor 2) with writeThrough {'enabled','locked'}, so the library-less build still writes its composed rows ⚠

- **Effort** L · **Depends on** RV-PF, PF-07, PF-02, LK-23, PF-00 · **Issues** PartyFrameEnhanced#14
- **Findings** —
- **Change** Re-apply the rolled-back adoption (issue #14; design in Ka0sAddonsCommonTasks/docs/2026-09-22-SUITE_STANDARDS_AND_LIBKA0S_SWEEP/3b-specs/schema.md:507-518) with the WS-02 route (a). settings/Schema.lua becomes the setup file: `local SchemaLib = LibStub and LibStub('LibKa0s-Schema-1.0', true) or HostSchemaStub` where HostSchemaStub is the runtime-completing stub from LibKa0s docs/api/Schema/version-2-docs.md 'The degradation stub' (copied from LibKa0s tests/test_schema.lua referenceStub, trimmed to what PFE calls, carrying SetMany and honoring row.normalize, with a comment naming that section), which takes the same writeThrough list and stores a row-less path in it (copy written at the path) while refusing every other row-less path. `inst = SchemaLib:New{ rows = NS.Schema, resolveRoot = function() return NS.db and NS.db.profile, 1 end, announce = function(row) if not row.sessionOnly and not row.writeThrough then publishConfig(sectionOf(row)) end end, debug = NS.Debug, debugEnabled = function() return NS.State and NS.State.debug end, format = NS.FormatSchemaValue, print = NS.Print, resetExempt = { [MINIMAP_PATH] = true }, writeThrough = { 'enabled', 'locked' } }` (the paths written by settings/Slash.lua:117 and :152 and modules/Preview.lua:134, plus the combat re-lock). Bind: NS.RegisterSchemaRows = function(rows) inst.AddRows(rows) end; NS.FindSchemaRow = inst.FindRow; NS.SetByPath = inst.Set; NS.ApplyDefault = inst.ApplyDefault; NS.Bulk.Begin/End/Run = inst.BulkBegin/BulkEnd/BulkRun; NS.ProfileRowsOffDefault = function() return inst.CountOffDefault(profilePred) end; NS.ResetProfileCounted = function(db) inst.ResetCounted(function() db:ResetProfile() end, profilePred) end; NS.ConsumeResetCount = inst.ConsumeResetCount; NS.ValidateSchema = function() return inst.Validate{ pages = VALID_PAGES, defaultsRoot = NS.defaults and NS.defaults.profile } end; NS.ResolvePath/NS.SetPath = SchemaLib.Read/SchemaLib.Write; NS.GetSetting = `local v = inst.Get(path); if v ~= nil then return v end; return SchemaLib.Read(NS.defaults and NS.defaults.profile, path)`. Delete byPath, the host bracket, SetSetting, the session and global registries: settings/General.lua stamps `row.get`/`row.set` onto the composed debug-console and minimap rows by path (the bodies of the current RegisterSessionSetting/RegisterGlobalSetting specs), and NS.IsGlobalSetting survives only as the source of profilePred and resetExempt. The `'%s refused'` debug line goes (a refused write is not a mutation). The `locked` row's AcceptLock stays in row.validate (the gate-in-validate rule). settings/OptionsSetup.lua keeps call-time forwarders for `set` and `applyDefault` (tests/test_optionssetup.lua:12-31 observes the Reset All veto through NS.ApplyDefault); settings/Slash.lua's descriptor keeps its wrappers, and `set` keeps returning nothing (AcceptLock prints its own one line, so Slash minor 15's CliSet refusal print would double it). Publish `NS.__schema = inst` for parity. tests/run.lua setSurfaceSource: add `['LibKa0s-Schema-1.0'] = mocks.LibStub('LibKa0s-Schema-1.0', true)`. docs/schema.md and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md Settings Schema: the seam is the library's; the writeThrough list and why; Known Limitations: on a library-less build `/pfe enable\|disable\|lock\|unlock` store the switch without the row's onChange (unchanged from today). Commit body: 'Closes #14'.  Upstream follow-up LK-23 made explicit: this item is the PartyFrameEnhanced#14 re-application LK-23's adoption notes name. It needs LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 minor 2 with `writeThrough` in the vendored v1.56.0 payload (RV-PF), and the host stub takes the same list. The list {'enabled','locked'} covers every path the follow-up names: settings/Slash.lua runEnabled (:152, `NS.SetByPath("enabled", on)`), runLock (:117, `NS.SetByPath("locked", locked)`), and modules/Preview.lua forceLock (:134, `NS.SetByPath("locked", true)`, the combat/master-switch/perf re-lock). Done when tests/test_schema.lua:209 ('without the library, /pfe disable and /pfe enable still write the stored path') is green on the adopted seam.
- **Test first** Characterize first (green on the host seam, must stay green): tests/test_schema.lua:209 ('without the library, /pfe disable and /pfe enable still write the stored path'); add beside it degraded `/pfe lock` / `/pfe unlock` writing `locked`, and a degraded write to a row-less path NOT in writeThrough ('general.__nope') refused and not stored. Then adopt and re-pin the deliberate deltas per the Schema adoption notes: unknown path refused on the live build (re-point test_schema.lua:170-181's bracket probe from 'general.__probe' to a real row path), table values copied into the store, no '%s refused' line. New parity cases in tests/test_surface_parity.lua: `T.assertSurfaceParity(NS.__schema, NS2.__schema, 'schema instance vs host stub')` and `T.assertSurfaceParity(NS2.__schemaLib, 'LibKa0s-Schema-1.0', { 'STRINGS' })` (publish NS.__schemaLib = SchemaLib). tests/perf.lua resolveUnchanged must stay 0.0 B/iter and within 24. Regenerate docs/test-cases.md and the badge.
- **Verify** Standard gate: ~/.claude/wow-addon/bin/ka0s-bounded luacheck . #### PF-13 — Minimap row's CLI path reads in its own shown sense: global.minimap.shown, inverting onto the stored minimap.hide ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** RV-PF, PF-11, WS-06, PF-00
- **Findings** —
- **Change** settings/General.lua:51 MINIMAP_PATH = 'global.minimap.shown'; the row's get/set closures (stamped in PF-11) keep inverting onto `NS.db.global.minimap.hide` and calling NS.Launcher:SetShown; no `shown` key is ever stored (anti-pattern #81). settings/OptionsSetup.lua exemptFromReset and the Schema descriptor's resetExempt use the new path; settings/Schema.lua comments (:105, :318 equivalents after PF-11). Validation: the row is a closure row, so Validate's resolution check must not look for `global.minimap.shown` in defaults; exempt it the way sessionOnly rows are exempted (profilePred / the Validate spec) and pin the storage default separately. Docs: docs/schema.md Global rows, docs/settings-panel.md, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md launcher table 'Visibility' cell, README if it names the path. SavedVariables are unchanged.  Minimap rename carry-over (WS-06): the stored LibDBIcon key db.global.minimap.hide does NOT move, so every existing player keeps their setting with no SavedVariables migration and no schema-version bump (a stored `shown` key would violate anti-pattern #81). Pin it with a test written first: a legacy store with minimap = { hide = true, minimapPos = 200 } reads `<slash> get <path>.minimap.shown` as false, the button stays hidden, minimapPos is untouched, and no `shown` key is ever written to the raw SV after a set. Record the player-facing CLI rename (`...minimap.hide` -> `...minimap.shown`, old path answers unknown setting) in the commit body for the next Version History roll.

Re-point tests/test_schema.lua:183-191 to the new path. New case: `NS.defaults.global.minimap.hide ~= nil` (the storage default the validator no longer resolves). Regenerate docs/test-cases.md and the badge.

#### MM-03 — 'Always show yourself' pins the player into the rows the window actually draws, scroll-aware ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-MM, MM-00
- **Findings** MultiMeters-R-01
- **Change** (1) modules/Aggregator.lua, beside ApplyRowLimit (:1289-1314): add `function Aggregator.SelfPinIndex(entries, first, visible, rowsConfig)`. It is pure and allocation-free. It answers nil when `not (rowsConfig and rowsConfig.alwaysShowSelf)`, when visible < 1, or when an entry with isPlayer sits in entries[first .. first+visible-1]. Otherwise it answers the index of the first isPlayer entry outside that slice, above it or below it. Keep ApplyRowLimit's own pin. (2) modules/Window.lua Render (:1164-1221, CCN 14): the CCN must not rise, so add NO inline branch. Add a file-local helper above Render, `local function slotEntry(entries, i, pin, lastIndex)`, which answers `entries[pin], pin` when pin and i == lastIndex, else `entries[i], i`. Add a method `WindowProto:SelfPin(entries, offset, isDrill)`, which answers nil in a drill-down and otherwise Aggregator.SelfPinIndex(entries, 1 + offset, self.layout.maxRows, (self.config or {}).rows). In Render, compute `local pin = self:SelfPin(entries, offset, isDrill)` and `local lastIndex = offset + layout.maxRows` once before the loop. Inside the loop, `local entry, rank = slotEntry(entries, i, pin, lastIndex)` and `row:Update(entry, rank)`. The pinned row then shows the player's real rank if the name cell draws one. Window.lua goes 1424 -> about 1445. Aggregator.lua goes 1360 -> about 1385.

#### MM-07 — Every event registration goes through Core.SafeRegisterEvent with a rejected list reachable from /mm debug diag ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-MM, MM-00
- **Findings** MultiMeters-A-20
- **Change** (1) core/CoreSetup.lua. Live branch: publish NS.SafeRegisterEvent, NS.SafeRegisterUnitEvent and NS.SafeRegisterEvents = lib.<same> (LibKa0s-Core-1.0 minor 8, LK-11). Stub branch (:90-): one-rung bodies, as the Core version-8 doc's 'Degradation' note prescribes. `NS.SafeRegisterEvent = function(target, event, handler, rejected) local ok = pcall(target.RegisterEvent, target, event, handler); if not ok and rejected then <append once> end; return ok end`, with the Unit and plural forms the same shape and no front gate. (2) core/MultiMeters.lua: add a module-level `local EVENTS` array of {event, handler} for all 22 registrations at :149-213: the 21 bare ones plus PLAYER_IS_GLIDING_CHANGED. Keep each block's explanatory comment beside its entries. OnEnable resets `NS.State.rejectedEvents = {}`, then calls `NS.SafeRegisterEvents(self, EVENTS...)`, or loops NS.SafeRegisterEvent(self, e[1], e[2], NS.State.rejectedEvents) if the plural's array shape does not take per-event handlers. target stays `self`, so Disabled 3 and 9 still see AceEvent registrations. When #rejected > 0, emit one NS.Debug('Init', 'rejected events: %s', table.concat(rejected, ', ')). Delete registerIfValid (:124-132). Fix the :153-156 comment: an unknown name RAISES, which is why every registration is pcalled. (3) core/Diagnostics.lua: /mm debug diag prints 'rejected events: none' or the list (localized through L if the diag lines are). (4) docs/midnight-quirks.md: a short note on registration isolation and where to read the rejected list. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md ## Event subscriptions: one sentence naming the helper (events-frames-taint-§1).

(c) A disable/enable cycle resets the list rather than appending duplicates. tests/test_degraded.lua: (d) with no library and a bad event, the addon still enables end to end, and the stub records the name. tests/test_diagnostics.lua: (e) diag prints the rejected line.

#### MM-12 — Migration stamp per savedvariables-§1 v2.65.0: defaults declare schemaVersion 0, the runner owns the stamp ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-MM, WS-03, MM-00
- **Findings** —
- **Change** WS-03 adoption. (1) defaults/Profile.lua:818: `schemaVersion = 0`. Rewrite the :717 doc to say why: AceDB strips a stored value equal to its default, and backfills a declared default onto an unstamped legacy account, and 0 has neither problem. (2) core/Database.lua RunMigrations (:933-): `local v = tonumber(g.schemaVersion) or 0; if v < 1 then v = 1 end`. 0 means unstamped, a legacy or fresh account. v1 is the shipped shape, and every step is idempotent against a fresh default profile, so both start at v1. Loop: `local step = migrations[v]`, `step(db)`, then `g.schemaVersion = v + 1` (the runner advances the stamp only after the step returned without raising) and log. Delete the 15 `db.global.schemaVersion = N` lines inside migrations[1..14] (:346 ... :928). Keep the missing-step fallthrough, but log it. (3) Profile-scoped steps already walk every stored profile through allProfiles(db) (:271); keep that, and state the savedvariables-§1 rule in the runner's header comment (:245-255). Fix the :117 comment ('backfills schemaVersion to the CURRENT value') to describe the 0 default. (4) Expose `Database.__migrations = migrations` as a documented test seam if none exists.
- **Test first** First: a new suite tests/test_migrations.lua, registered in tests/run.lua after test_database (which is at 1470). (a) NS.defaults.global.schemaVersion == 0, which is red today. (b) A fresh install with no stored stamp runs every step and ends at CURRENT_DB_VERSION, and the seeded profile deep-equals the one a migration-free InitDB builds, so every step is idempotent against fresh defaults. (c) A step that raises leaves the stamp at its `from` value: replace Database.__migrations[3] with an erroring function for one run, then restore it in a guaranteed-run wrapper. (d) After the walk, the raw sv.global.schemaVersion is CURRENT and survives the kit 26 AceDB fake's default stripping. (e) A legacy v1-shaped account with no stamp, on two profiles, migrates both profiles.
- **Verify** ka0s-bounded luacheck . (no output); git ls-files '*.lua' \| grep -v -e '^libs/' -e '^tests/_kit/' \| xargs wc -l \| awk '$2!="total" && $1>1500' (no output) (no output); grep -c 'db.global.schemaVersion = ' core/Database.lua (0)

#### MM-14 — Adopt LibKa0s-Schema-1.0 minor 2 for the settings runtime: Set with window instanceId, SetMany with one announce, the registry and the bracket; delete the host copies (MultiMeters#52) ⚠

- **Effort** L · **Depends on** RV-MM, MM-09, MM-13, LK-22, MM-00 · **Issues** MultiMeters#52
- **Findings** —
- **Change** settings/Schema_Paths.lua (972 lines). (1) `local SchemaLib = LibStub and LibStub("LibKa0s-Schema-1.0", true)` and one instance `S = SchemaLib:New{ rows = NS.Schema, resolveRoot = resolveRoot, announce = ..., announceBatch = ..., debug = ..., debugEnabled = ..., resetExempt = { [MINIMAP_PATH] = true }, L = ... }`. resolveRoot is the existing :179 function, reshaped to `function(parts, windowId) -> root, first, resolvedWindowId`, so the 'window.' prefix resolves against the id handed in or State.activeWindowId. announce(row, path, value, rid) runs announceWrite(row.page, rid). announceBatch(writes, rid) sends CONFIG_CHANGED ONCE, through announceWrite with the common page/id or nil, exactly as SetByPaths does at :786-795. (2) Delete the host primitives splitPath/readFrom/writeInto/copy (:97-132), the index/reindex/FindSchemaRow/RegisterSchemaRows registry (:245-280) and the bulk bracket bulkOpen/bulkClose/runBulk/sameValue/bulkBegin/bulkEnd/bulkRun (:549-634). The public names stay as shims so no caller moves: NS.FindSchemaRow = S:FindRow, NS.RegisterSchemaRows = S:AddRows, NS.GetSetting(path, id) = S:Get(path, id), NS.SetByPath(path, v, id) = S:Set(path, v, id), NS.ApplyDefault(row, id) = S:ApplyDefault(row, id), and NS.Bulk = { begin = S.BulkBegin, finish = S.BulkEnd, run = S.BulkRun } for settings/OptionsSetup.lua:258-263. NS.SetByPaths(writes, windowId, summary) maps each { path, value } onto { path =, value = } and calls S:SetMany(entries, { instanceId = windowId, act = <summary act>, scope = <summary scope> }). Split `summary` so the bracket line reads as today's (characterize it first). (3) The columns carve-out becomes a hidden row { path = 'window.columns', hidden = true, validate = <structural check>, normalize = normalizeColumns }, using row.normalize from Schema minor 2. The in-array refusal ('window.columns.2.width') stays, because no row matches that path. (4) The minimap carve-out moves into the row's own get/set closures. The composer builds the row from Schema_Compose.lua's decor at :786, so the closures go there and resolve NS.db.global.minimap at call time. They keep TODAY's polarity: the row value means shown, and the store is hide = not v plus the LibDBIcon refresh. MM-16 renames the path. #### MM-15 — Library-absent /mm enable and /mm disable write through the Schema seam's writeThrough list (options-ui-§1 v2.65.0 route (a))

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-MM, MM-14, MM-00

(1) settings/Schema_Paths.lua: the SchemaLib:New descriptor, and the degradation stub from MM-14, both get `writeThrough = { "enabled" }` as data. `enabled` is the only composed Master-controls row a host verb writes: /mm lock writes each window's hand-written frame.locked row, and /mm test goes through WindowManager, so neither needs writeThrough (check with grep for CliSet/SetByPath on composed paths). The stub stores a writeThrough path raw at db.profile.enabled and refuses every other row-less path. announce: when `row.writeThrough and path == "enabled"`, call NS.SyncEnabledHold(). The composed row's onChange (Schema_Compose.lua:712-724) is absent on these loads, so this is the latch reaction. (2) settings/Slash.lua doEnabled (:406-408): when the Slash library is absent (a file-local `degraded` flag set in the stub branch), call `local ok, err = NS.SetByPath("enabled", want)` and print L["enabled = %s"]:format(tostring(want)) on success, or err. It never raises. The live path keeps cli:CliSet. (3) docs/ARCHITECTURE.md: record that enable/disable take WS-02 route (a) on a library-absent or partial load, and replace any register row that said the degraded enable prints 'set is unavailable'.
- **Test first** First, red, in tests/test_degraded.lua (716). (a) With the library absent, `/mm disable` stores enabled=false, NS.IsDisabled() is true, every registration is gone (Disabled-3 style), and no Lua error. Red today: it prints '/mm set is unavailable' and stores nothing. (b) `/mm enable` then restores registrations. (c) A partial load (libFiles without Options*.lua, Schema present) gives the same result as (a), through the live instance's writeThrough. (d) `/mm set some.unknown x` on the stub is refused, never stored.

#### MM-16 — Minimap row's CLI path reads in its own shown sense: global.minimap.shown, storage unchanged at minimap.hide ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-MM, MM-14, WS-06, MM-12, MM-00
- **Findings** —
- **Change** WS-06 (launcher-§3 v2.65.0). settings/Schema_Paths.lua:222 MINIMAP_PATH = "global.minimap.shown". settings/Schema_Compose.lua:658 minimapPath = "global.minimap.shown". :786 decor key "global.minimap.shown", whose get/set closures (from MM-14) invert onto db.global.minimap.hide. Update the :780-785 and Schema_Paths :210-221 comments. There is no SavedVariables change and no migration: the stored key stays LibDBIcon's `hide`, and no `shown` key is ever stored (anti-pattern #81). settings/OptionsSetup.lua's reset exemption reads NS.MINIMAP_PATH and follows automatically. Sweep every other 'global.minimap.hide' path mention (63 hits across code, tests and docs outside the frozen bundles): docs/schema.md, docs/settings-panel.md, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, README.md and the tests.  SavedVariables continuity for existing players (WS-06): only the CLI and schema path is renamed. The stored key stays db.global.minimap.hide (LibDBIcon's), which the v15 migration in core/Database.lua (:52, :850-891) already moved to the global store. An existing player's choice therefore carries over as it is, and no data migration step or CURRENT_DB_VERSION bump is added, because a stored `shown` key is forbidden (anti-pattern #81). Pin this with extra tests in tests/test_schema_batch.lua. (e) A legacy account loaded with db.global.minimap.hide=true reads `/mm get global.minimap.shown` as false, and the button stays hidden after load. (f) A pre-v15 account with profile.minimap.hide=true, run through the migration runner, also reads shown=false, and no `shown` key exists in the raw SV. If the implementer finds any path string persisted in SavedVariables (a saved reset-exempt list, a bookmark or similar), STOP and raise it with the owner: this plan adds no SavedVariables migration for the rename.  Minimap rename carry-over (WS-06): the stored LibDBIcon key db.global.minimap.hide does NOT move, so every existing player keeps their setting with no SavedVariables migration and no schema-version bump (a stored `shown` key would violate anti-pattern #81). Pin it with a test written first: a legacy store with minimap = { hide = true, minimapPos = 200 } reads `<slash> get <path>.minimap.shown` as false, the button stays hidden, minimapPos is untouched, and no `shown` key is ever written to the raw SV after a set. Record the player-facing CLI rename (`...minimap.hide` -> `...minimap.shown`, old path answers unknown setting) in the commit body for the next Version History roll.  PLAN-REVIEW CORRECTION: correct cites: CURRENT_DB_VERSION is core/Database.lua:53, and the step that moves minimap to the global store is migrations[14] (:895-935; the runner indexes by from-version). Test (f) seeds schemaVersion 14.

#### LH-01 — Clear the Mythic+ keystone context when the player leaves the party instance or the key resets ⚠

- **Effort** M · **Depends on** RV-LH, LH-00
- **Findings** LootHistory-R-01
- **Change** core/Compat.lua (beside GetActiveKeystoneLevel, :27-34): add `function Compat.InPartyInstance()`. It returns false when `IsInInstance` is not a function; otherwise `local inInst, kind = IsInInstance(); return (inInst and kind == "party") and true or false`. modules/Attribution.lua: (1) new `Attribution:OnZoneChanged()`. When `NS.Compat.InPartyInstance()` is false, set `State.keystone = nil`. When it is true and State.keystone is nil, read `local lvl = NS.Compat.GetActiveKeystoneLevel()` and set `State.keystone = { level = lvl }` only when `lvl and lvl > 0`; this re-arms a key the player zoned back into, because CHALLENGE_MODE_START does not fire again. Each branch writes a debug-gated `NS.Debug("Attr", ...)` line. (2) New `Attribution:OnChallengeModeReset()`: `State.keystone = nil` plus a debug line. (3) OnChallengeModeCompleted (:235-243): `local lvl = NS.Compat.GetActiveKeystoneLevel(); if lvl and lvl > 0 then State.keystone.level = lvl end`, which replaces the `or` at :238 so a completion-time 0 is never stored. (4) Enable (:346-359): register `ZONE_CHANGED_NEW_AREA` -> OnZoneChanged and `CHALLENGE_MODE_RESET` -> OnChallengeModeReset on `bus`, and append both names to `self.__events` (nine names), so Disable tears them down by name. Do NOT register PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD on NS.addon: core/LifecycleSetup.lua:112 binds OnEnterWorld there. (5) Disable's docstring (:401-404) says nine shared-target events. .luacheckrc read_globals: add "IsInInstance". tests/test_attribution.lua: ENABLE_EVENTS (:348-351) gains CHALLENGE_MODE_RESET and ZONE_CHANGED_NEW_AREA (kept sorted); the case at :396 is renamed from 'seven' to 'nine'; the comments at :333 and :345 say nine. tests/test_disabled.lua OWNED (:69-86) gains "event:ZONE_CHANGED_NEW_AREA:nil" and "event:CHALLENGE_MODE_RESET:nil". Docs: docs/data-flow.md:88 and docs/midnight-quirks.md:22 describe the lifetime (kept through completion, cleared on leaving the party instance or on CHALLENGE_MODE_RESET, re-armed on re-entry to an active key). docs/ARCHITECTURE.md ## Event subscriptions gains both events. The performance-§12 register row (ARCHITECTURE.md:485, 'all thirteen registrations') and the docs/performance.md sweep count move to fifteen, and each new handler is described as one API call per zone change. The commit body notes that existing mislabelled MPLUS rows cannot be repaired, because no signal exists.
- **Test first** Test first, in tests/test_attribution.lua. Five new cases, all red today because the handlers do not exist (a nil method call) or because of the `or` at :238. (a) With State.keystone={level=12} and M.__context.inInstance=false, OnZoneChanged() is followed by ResolveLootSource(OBJECT) -> CONTAINER. (b) With inInstance=true and instanceType='party', the keystone is kept -> MPLUS 12. (c) OnChallengeModeReset() clears it. (d) A GetActiveKeystoneInfo mock answering 0 at completion leaves level 12. (e) Re-entry: keystone nil, a party instance, and GetActiveKeystoneInfo answering 15 means OnZoneChanged re-arms level 15. Save and restore M.__context and C_ChallengeMode in each case. Then update the Enable case to nine events and test_disabled's OWNED list; both are red until Enable registers the two events.

#### LH-02 — Keep the encounter context for a grace window after a successful ENCOUNTER_END, so boss-corpse loot carries encounterID ⚠

- **Effort** S · **Depends on** RV-LH, LH-01, LH-00
- **Findings** LootHistory-R-02
- **Change** core/Constants.lua (beside C.CONTEXT_TTL, :67): `C.ENCOUNTER_GRACE = 60`, with a comment saying the corpse is looted after ENCOUNTER_END and that trash KILL loot within the window also carries the id (accepted). modules/Attribution.lua: the ENCOUNTER_END registration (:351) forwards varargs: `function(...) self:OnEncounterEnd(...) end`. OnEncounterEnd(_, encounterID, _, _, _, success) (:223-226): when `success == 1 and State.encounter`, it sets `State.encounter.expires = GetTime() + Constants.ENCOUNTER_GRACE`; otherwise (a wipe, or no context) it sets `State.encounter = nil`. The debug line names the outcome. ResolveLootSource (:163-180): `local enc = state.encounter; if enc and (enc.expires == nil or (state.now or GetTime()) <= enc.expires) then detail.encounterID = enc.id; detail.difficulty = enc.difficulty end`. The pure resolver takes `now` through state so tests do not depend on the clock. OnEncounterStart still replaces the table, so a new pull drops the old expiry. Docs: docs/data-flow.md:87 and docs/midnight-quirks.md:17 describe the grace window and the wipe clear.

#### LH-03 — Rebuild the currency-category cache once per missed id instead of freezing the first session snapshot ⚠

Compat.CurrencyCategory(currencyID): after the lazy first build, `local h = currencyCategoryCache[currencyID]`. #### LH-04 — Confirm before a shorter 'Keep history for' deletes records; declining restores the previous retention ⚠

- **Findings** LootHistory-R-04
- **Change** core/Database.lua (beside PruneOld, :786): add `Database:CountOlderThan(days)`. It answers 0 for nil or 0 days; otherwise it counts history rows with (r.ts or 0) < time() - days*86400, allocation-free. settings/Slash.lua StaticPopupDialogs block (:7-80): add `KA0S_LOOTHISTORY_PRUNE`. Its text is `Shorten 'Keep history for' to %s? %s older records will be deleted. This cannot be undone.`, with button1 YES, button2 NO, timeout 0, whileDead, hideOnEscape, showAlert and preferredIndex 3, matching the house pair. OnAccept(_, data) calls `NS.Schema:ConfirmRetention(data.days, true)`. OnCancel(_, data) calls `NS.Schema:ConfirmRetention(data.days, false)`. settings/Schema.lua: add a module-local `confirmedRetention`, `function S:SyncRetention()` (reads NS.db.global.settings.retentionDays into it), and `function S:ConfirmRetention(days, accepted)`. On accept it runs NS.Database:PruneOld() and sets confirmedRetention = days. On decline it sets a module-local `restoring = true`, calls `S:Set("settings.retentionDays", confirmedRetention)`, sets `restoring = false`, and prints one line: `retention kept at <label>; no records were deleted.` The row's onChange (:370-375) becomes `function(value) S:OnRetentionChanged(value) end`. OnRetentionChanged: return while restoring. `local n = NS.Database:CountOlderThan(value)`. When n == 0, set confirmedRetention = value and return. When StaticPopup_Show is not a function, run PruneOld, set confirmedRetention = value and return. Otherwise `StaticPopup_Show("KA0S_LOOTHISTORY_PRUNE", label, n, { days = value })`, where label is the RETENTION_OPTIONS text. core/LootHistory.lua OnInitialize calls `NS.Schema:SyncRetention()` right after NS:InitDB(). Sl:ResetEverything (settings/Slash.lua:180-200) calls it after wipeGlobal, because the raw wipe fires no onChange. The login prune (core/LootHistory.lua:81-84) is unchanged, because the stored value is already confirmed. docs/settings-panel.md (History tab): describe the confirm and the decline-restores behavior. The row keeps no options-ui-§12 citation.

History still holds 5 rows; the spy saw 'KA0S_LOOTHISTORY_PRUNE' with n=3 and data.days=7. Red today: the onChange prunes immediately. (b) Accept: StaticPopupDialogs.KA0S_LOOTHISTORY_PRUNE.OnAccept(nil, {days=7}) leaves 2 rows. (c) Decline: after SyncRetention at 30, Set 7 then OnCancel(nil, {days=7}). retentionDays reads 30 again, 5 rows remain, and exactly one line is printed.
